362-| Approach | Trade-off |
363-|---|---|
364-| Same unit for both | Simplest; fragments may lack context |
365:| **Neighbour expansion — chosen** | Retrieve the chunk, send it plus adjacent siblings. No side-store, predictable token cost |
366-| Parent-section expansion | Best grounding, but needs parent text stored outside the vector payload, and one large section can blow the latency budget |
367-
368-Parent expansion was the initial choice and then dropped: it requires keeping
369-section text in a side store (storing it per-chunk would duplicate a section
370-once per child), plus a size cap and a fallback for oversized sections.
371-Neighbour expansion gets most of the grounding benefit with none of that, since
372-neighbours are just other chunks already in Qdrant, fetched by a payload filter
373-on (`doc_id`, `chunk_index` range) — no extra embedding work at query time. It
374-does need overlapping windows merged, and the window clamped to `parent_id` so a
375-neighbour from a different section can't mislead grounding.
376-
377-This composes well with Claude's native citations: citations resolve to spans

Deferred, with the trigger made explicit rather than left as "later."

**`docs/work.md`** — removed from RAG — Store's outstanding list, added to Deferred with the reasoning: retrieval is 18/18 on the golden set, so there's no failure for expansion to fix, and it would roughly triple context tokens to buy that. The implementation notes are kept with it (keyed `retrieve()` via `uuid5` ids, merge windows, clamp to `parent_id`, and the `point_id()` move out of `index.py` as a prerequisite) so nothing has to be re-derived when it's justified.

**`docs/considerations.md`** — the decision table now reads "chosen, not built," and the section states the trigger: an eval case that answers wrongly *because the chunk was a fragment*. It also notes `parent_id` is already in the payload, so the work can start immediately when that appears.

This puts it on the same footing as the captioner and re-ranking — measured failure, not preference — which is a consistent story rather than three separate ad-hoc deferrals.

That makes the **eval harness** the thing standing between you and every remaining judgement call: it's what produces the failing cases for expansion, the `image_only` results that decide the captioner, and the A/B for `REWRITE_KEYWORDS_ENABLED` / `REWRITE_SUB_QUERIES_ENABLED`. Want to plan that next?

```
- ~~Generate the later batch of 5 docs~~ (`corpus/docs-later/`) — new topics, no contradictions; 5 `batch: later` golden questions must be declined before ingestion and answered after

## RAG — Read (ingestion)

Lives in `app/rag/ingest/`. Produces an enriched `DoclingDocument` per document; chunking and indexing belong to RAG — Store.

**Discovery and state**

--- Done ---
- ~~Write all of a document's points in a **single upsert**~~ — atomically indexed at a hash or not at all; `state.py` reads that invariant back
- ~~Act on the plan~~ — index `new` + `changed`, skip `unchanged`, delete the vectors of `deleted`; all four verified end to end against the running stack
- ~~Recursive walk for `.pdf`/`.docx`/`.html` at any depth, stable ordering~~ (`app/rag/ingest/discovery.py`) — skips dotfiles and the golden answer key
- ~~`doc_id` = path relative to the corpus root; `doc_content_hash` = SHA-256 of file bytes~~
- ~~Work out what a run has to do~~ (`app/rag/ingest/state.py`) — read from the collection rather than a side file, so the record cannot disagree with the index; new/changed/unchanged/deleted, with a multi-hash document treated as a partial write and re-ingested
- ~~Detect documents that disappeared~~ — they appear as `deleted` in the plan

**Parse**

--- Done ---
- ~~One Docling `DocumentConverter`, OCR on, table structure on, per-format options~~ (`app/rag/ingest/parse.py`) — built once per process; `generate_picture_images` on, or figure images are discarded and the caption stage has nothing to work with
- ~~Wrap in a `ParsedDocument`~~ — `doc_id`, source format, content hash, page/table/picture counts and parse duration
- ~~A document that fails to convert is logged at ERROR and skipped~~ — one malformed file must not block the corpus; it stays un-indexed and is retried next run. **Production would alert on these ERROR lines** so a document that never parses cannot fail silently
- ~~No `lang` field~~ — nothing supplies it for free and BGE-M3 is multilingual, so retrieval does not need it
- ~~`TORCHDYNAMO_DISABLE=1` before torch is imported~~ — set in every module that reaches torch; without a C compiler in the image, every conversion fails otherwise
- ~~`page_no` only ever populates for PDF~~ — DOCX and HTML chunks carry an empty page list, and citations must tolerate that rather than treat it as an error
- ~~A page-spanning table arrives as **two** table objects~~ — accepted, not worked around: each half chunks as a complete-looking table with its header repeated

**Image descriptions** — *deferred, revisit after the pipeline is end-to-end.* Text and tables carry most of the corpus; images are an improvement on top. The `image_only` golden questions retrieve the right document today but nothing describes the figures, so the answers are not derivable yet.

Blocking facts from the spikes, all of which this pass must handle:
- HTML figures carry **no image bytes** — resolve the `<img src>` against the document's directory and load the file ourselves, or HTML figures can never be described
- DOCX captions are **not associated** by Docling — accept generation for every DOCX figure, or parse the DOCX XML for the caption paragraph
- A PDF `caption` can be **OCR'd text from inside the image** rather than the real caption — trust it as `extracted`, or gate it (e.g. require a `Figure`/`Abbildung` prefix)
- Write descriptions to `PictureItem.meta` (`PictureMeta.description`, `created_by` carries provenance) — `annotations` works but is deprecated in docling-core 2.91

The work itself:
- Caption-first: `PictureItem.captions` present → provenance `extracted`
- Generate for the gaps as our own pipeline stage (Docling's enrichment is PDF-only), using Docling's default picture-description model
- Record provenance `extracted` / `generated` per image
- Cache generated captions by image hash, in a gitignored cache directory
- Write descriptions back into the `DoclingDocument` **before** chunking
- Revisit the captioner if the `image_only` golden questions fail: a generic caption ("a line chart") cannot answer qa-005, which needs the figure read factually. Claude vision is the fallback and the trigger is measured failure, not preference

**Orchestration**
- Extend the run summary with picture counts and captions extracted vs generated once the image stage exists

--- Done ---
- ~~Ingestion is a **job, not a service**~~ — it runs to completion, is started by an operator or a schedule, and takes minutes; that is a batch job, and it needs no network surface at all
- ~~`run()` ties discovery, plan and parse together~~ (`app/rag/ingest/runner.py`) — returns a `RunReport`; indexing and vector deletion are a marked seam for RAG — Store, and the report does not claim documents were indexed
- ~~`python -m app.rag.ingest` over the `CORPUS_DIR` setting~~ (`app/rag/ingest/__main__.py`) — `--force` re-processes everything, `--dry-run` prints the plan and stops. No directory argument: the root is a setting, so a run cannot be aimed at arbitrary host files
- ~~Exit non-zero when a document failed to parse~~ — a scheduled run must not report success over a partially indexed corpus
- ~~The ingester container is a one-shot job~~ — `docker compose run --rm ingest`, behind an `ingest` profile so `docker compose up` does not start it; no port, no healthcheck, no server

**Tests**
- Unit: caption cache — fixtures only, no models
- Integration over two real corpus files, marked and kept out of the default suite like `e2e`

--- Done ---
- ~~Spike: does `PictureItem.image` yield image bytes for DOCX and HTML?~~ (`scripts/spikes/docling_pictures.py`) — PDF yes, DOCX yes, **HTML no**; DOCX captions are never associated, and a PDF caption can be OCR'd in-image text
- ~~Spike: how does a picture reach the chunker?~~ (`scripts/spikes/docling_chunking.py`) — pictures do become chunks (merged with surrounding prose), `contextualize()` prepends the heading path, and a description on `PictureItem.meta` reaches the chunk text
- ~~Add a DOCX figure to the corpus~~ — no DOCX carried an image, so the format's description path was untestable; `accessory-catalogue-en.docx` now has a captioned figure and `troubleshooting-zeitplan-de.docx` an uncaptioned one

## RAG — Store

--- Done ---
- ~~Use Qdrant's embedded mode in unit tests~~ — `:memory:` throughout the collection, index and retriever tests, so real retrieval is exercised without Docker. Payload indexes are the one thing it ignores, so that assertion checks the call and was verified against the real server
- ~~Chunk with `HybridChunker`~~ (`app/rag/ingest/chunk.py`) — BGE-M3's own tokenizer at `max_tokens=512`, table header repeated on overflow, peers merged. The tokenizer must be the embedder's: a chunk sized in someone else's tokens overflows silently at embed time
```

RAG — Read now matches every other section: heading, outstanding items, `--- Done ---`, done items. No intro paragraph, no sub-headings.

It went from five sub-blocks and ~35 lines to **3 outstanding + 20 done**. What changed beyond restructuring:

- The image work compressed from a paragraph plus two sub-lists into **one bullet** carrying the approach, the three spike findings it must handle, and the Claude-vision fallback.
- The `--- Done ---` items are ordered by pipeline stage — discovery → state → parse → orchestration → spikes — so it reads as the order things were built.
- **"Integration over two real corpus files" was outstanding but is actually done** — `test_parse_documents.py` and `test_chunk_documents.py` do exactly that behind the `docling` marker. Moved down.
- The `run()` entry said it "ties discovery, plan and parse together" with indexing as "a marked seam" — stale since the runner now does the full pipeline. Corrected.

The only remaining structural oddity in the file is the "Tooling not set up yet" sub-block inside Deferred, which I'd leave — it's a genuine grouping rather than a stage, and flattening it would bury six items in a longer list.

# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1
#
# The api image. Deliberately excludes Docling and everything it drags in —
# parsing and chunking belong to the ingester, and no request path touches them
# (`uv sync --no-default-groups` installs main dependencies only).

FROM python:3.14-slim AS base

COPY --from=ghcr.io/astral-sh/uv:latest /uv /bin/uv

ENV PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 \
    PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 \
    UV_COMPILE_BYTECODE=1 \
    UV_LINK_MODE=copy \
    # Outside /app so a bind-mounted working tree cannot shadow the venv.
    UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT=/opt/venv

WORKDIR /app


FROM base AS deps

COPY pyproject.toml uv.lock ./
# --no-install-project: dependencies only, so this layer is cached until the
# lockfile changes rather than on every source edit.
RUN --mount=type=cache,target=/root/.cache/uv \
    uv sync --frozen --no-default-groups --no-install-project


FROM base AS runtime

COPY --from=deps /opt/venv /opt/venv
ENV PATH="/opt/venv/bin:$PATH"

COPY app ./app

# BGE-M3 is baked in rather than fetched on first use: the api embeds the query
# before every search, so a first request that downloads 2.3 GB is not a warm-up
# cost, it is a timeout. Deliberately not under /models — that used to be a
# volume mount point, and a mounted volume shadows whatever the image carries.
ENV HF_HOME=/opt/models/huggingface

# The placeholders exist because `Settings` requires the runtime secrets and the
# image carries no `.env`. Scoped to this RUN, so nothing enters a layer.
RUN ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=build JWT_SECRET=build \
    python -c "from app.embedding import embed_documents; embed_documents(['warmup'])"

# The model directory stays root-owned — it is read-only at runtime.
RUN useradd --create-home --uid 1000 appuser \
    && mkdir -p /app/logs \
    && chown -R appuser:appuser /app
USER appuser

EXPOSE 8000

HEALTHCHECK --interval=15s --timeout=5s --start-period=20s --retries=3 \
    CMD python -c "import urllib.request; urllib.request.urlopen('http://127.0.0.1:8000/').read()"

CMD ["uvicorn", "app.main:app", "--host", "0.0.0.0", "--port", "8000"]
# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1
#
# The ingester image — a one-shot job, run with `docker compose run --rm ingest`.
# Carries Docling and everything it drags in — torch, transformers, the
# layout/table models and OCR — which is exactly why it is a separate image from
# the api. Expect several GB.

FROM python:3.14-slim AS base

COPY --from=ghcr.io/astral-sh/uv:latest /uv /bin/uv

ENV PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 \
    PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 \
    UV_COMPILE_BYTECODE=1 \
    UV_LINK_MODE=copy \
    # Outside /app so a bind-mounted working tree cannot shadow the venv.
    UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT=/opt/venv \
    # torch JIT-compiles through inductor/triton, which needs a C compiler that
    # is not here. Ingest is offline, so the eager path costs nothing that
    # matters — without this, every conversion fails.
    TORCHDYNAMO_DISABLE=1

WORKDIR /app


FROM base AS deps

COPY pyproject.toml uv.lock ./
RUN --mount=type=cache,target=/root/.cache/uv \
    uv sync --frozen --no-default-groups --group ingest --no-install-project


FROM base AS runtime

# Docling's PDF backend pulls in opencv-python, which links against X11 shared
# libraries that the slim image does not carry. Without these every PDF fails to
# convert with `ImportError: libxcb.so.1`; DOCX and HTML are unaffected, so the
# gap shows up as a partially indexed corpus rather than an obvious break.
RUN apt-get update \
    && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends libgl1 libglib2.0-0 libxcb1 \
    && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*

COPY --from=deps /opt/venv /opt/venv
ENV PATH="/opt/venv/bin:$PATH"

COPY app ./app

# Models are baked into the image rather than fetched on first use: the image
# digest then fully determines what the OCR and layout stages do, replicas start
# without racing each other to download, and nothing inside the runtime
# filesystem needs to be writable. Deliberately NOT under /models — that is a
# volume mount point, and a mounted volume would shadow whatever the image
# carries there.
ENV HF_HOME=/opt/models/huggingface

# Warm the converter on a real document, and the chunker's tokenizer alongside
# it: converting is the only way to be sure every model the runtime path asks
# for (layout, table structure, and RapidOCR's weights plus its character
# dictionary) is actually present.
COPY corpus/docs-initial/bulk/release-notes-4-1-de.pdf /tmp/warmup.pdf
#
# The placeholders exist because the chunker and embedder read `Settings`,
# which requires the runtime secrets — and the image deliberately carries no
# `.env`. They are scoped to this RUN, so nothing is written into a layer; the
# real values arrive from the environment at run time.
RUN ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=build JWT_SECRET=build python -c "\
from app.rag.ingest.parse import get_converter; \
from app.rag.ingest.chunk import get_chunker; \
from app.embedding import embed_documents; \
get_converter().convert('/tmp/warmup.pdf'); \
get_chunker(); \
embed_documents(['warmup'])" \
    && rm /tmp/warmup.pdf

# /corpus is a volume mount point: a named volume inherits the mount point's
# ownership, and root-owned would break the non-root user. The model directories
# stay root-owned — they are read-only at runtime, which is the point.
RUN useradd --create-home --uid 1000 appuser \
    && mkdir -p /app/logs /corpus \
    && chown -R appuser:appuser /app /corpus
USER appuser

# A job, not a service: it runs to completion and exits, so there is no port, no
# healthcheck and no server. The exit code is the result — non-zero if any
# document failed to parse.
ENTRYPOINT ["python", "-m", "app.rag.ingest"]
# Local development stack: api + qdrant as long-running services, ingestion as a
# one-shot job behind the `ingest` profile — see docs/work.md.
services:
  qdrant:
    # Keep in step with `qdrant-client` in pyproject.toml: the client warns and
    # may misbehave when the server's major/minor drift apart.
    image: qdrant/qdrant:v1.19.0
    restart: unless-stopped
    ports:
      # Exposed for inspection during development; the api reaches it over the
      # compose network regardless.
      - "6333:6333"
    volumes:
      - qdrant-storage:/qdrant/storage
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD-SHELL", "bash -c ':> /dev/tcp/127.0.0.1/6333'"]
      interval: 10s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 5
      start_period: 10s

  api:
    build:
      context: .
      dockerfile: Dockerfile.api
    restart: unless-stopped
    env_file: .env
    environment:
      # Service name on the compose network, not localhost.
      QDRANT_URL: http://qdrant:6333
      # The service appends its own subdirectory — see configure_logging.
      LOG_DIR: /app/logs
      # No HF_HOME and no model cache volume: BGE-M3 is baked into the image, and
      # a volume mounted over it would hide what the image carries.
    ports:
      - "8000:8000"
    volumes:
      # Development mount: source is live-editable and logs land on the host.
      # An upper environment would drop this and run the code baked into the
      # image — see docs/considerations.md.
      - ./app:/app/app
      - ./logs:/app/logs
    command:
      - uvicorn
      - app.main:app
      - --host
      - "0.0.0.0"
      - --port
      - "8000"
      - --reload
    depends_on:
      qdrant:
        condition: service_healthy

  # A job, not a service: it runs the pipeline to completion and exits.
  #
  #   docker compose run --rm ingest              # ingest what changed
  #   docker compose run --rm ingest --dry-run    # show the plan, do nothing
  #
  # The `ingest` profile keeps it out of `docker compose up`, which would
  # otherwise start it on every stack boot.
  ingest:
    profiles: ["ingest"]
    build:
      context: .
      dockerfile: Dockerfile.ingester
    env_file: .env
    environment:
      QDRANT_URL: http://qdrant:6333
      LOG_DIR: /app/logs
      CORPUS_DIR: /corpus
      # No HF_HOME and no model cache volume here: the ingester's models are
      # baked into its image, and a volume mounted over them would hide them.
    volumes:
      # The initial set is the ingestion root; the later batch is copied in here
      # to demonstrate incremental ingestion. Read-only: the ingester parses
      # documents, it never writes them.
      - ./corpus/docs-initial:/corpus:ro
      - ./app:/app/app
      - ./logs:/app/logs
    depends_on:
      qdrant:
        condition: service_healthy

volumes:
  qdrant-storage:
# The venv is ~5 GB and is rebuilt inside the image — never ship it as context.
.venv/
__pycache__/
*.pyc

.git/
.gitignore
.githooks/

.env
logs/
chat-logs/
.pytest_cache/

# Generated documents are mounted as a volume, not baked into the image. The one
# exception is the document the ingester build converts to pull its models down;
# it is deleted in the same layer.
corpus/
!corpus/docs-initial/bulk/release-notes-4-1-de.pdf

docs/
tests/
scripts/spikes/

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Run the app locally. Needs a .env with ANTHROPIC_API_KEY (see .env.example).
# Set RELOAD=1 to restart on code changes.
# set -euo pipefail

# cd "$(dirname "$0")"

HOST="${HOST:-127.0.0.1}"
PORT="${PORT:-8000}"

args=(uv run uvicorn app.main:app --host "$HOST" --port "$PORT")
[ "${RELOAD:-0}" = "1" ] && args+=(--reload)

exec "${args[@]}"

What would you like to clarify?

## user — 21:57

what's the recommended approach for naming convention of dockerfiles?

## assistant — 21:57

Two conventions are in real use:

- **`api.Dockerfile`** (suffix) — what Docker's own docs recommend when a project has more than one Dockerfile. Editors, syntax highlighting, and linters (hadolint, VS Code, JetBrains) match on the `*.Dockerfile` glob, so these get recognized automatically.
- **`Dockerfile.api`** (prefix) — older, still widespread. Tooling support is spottier; some editors need a manual file-association to highlight it.

Recommendation: **`docker/api.Dockerfile` + `docker/ingester.Dockerfile`**. It's the documented convention, it sorts the two files next to each other, and it keeps tooling working.

The third option — `docker/api/Dockerfile`, `docker/ingester/Dockerfile` — is only worth it if each service will grow its own sidecar files (entrypoint scripts, configs). You have none today, so it's an extra directory level for nothing.

Note that since you chose to move `docker-compose.yml` into `docker/` too, `.dockerignore` still has to stay at the repo root — it's resolved relative to the build context, which becomes `..`.

{
  "file_path": "/home/sebi/.claude/plans/hi-i-d-like-to-majestic-swan.md",
  "content": "# Group Dockerfiles under `docker/`\n\n## Context\n\nThe repo root carries `Dockerfile.api` and `Dockerfile.ingester` alongside the\ncompose file, source, docs and config. As the first step of a broader\nrefactoring pass, the two build files get grouped into a `docker/` directory so\nthe root listing reflects the project rather than its build plumbing.\n\n`docker-compose.yml` **stays at the repo root** \u2014 it is the entry point (`docker\ncompose up` with no `-f`), and moving it would force either a flag on every\ncommand or a `COMPOSE_FILE` env var. `.dockerignore` also stays at the root:\nDocker resolves it relative to the *build context*, which remains `.`, so moving\nit would silently disable it and ship the ~5 GB `.venv` as context.\n\nNaming follows Docker's documented convention for projects with more than one\nDockerfile \u2014 the `<purpose>.Dockerfile` suffix form, which editors, hadolint and\nIDE syntax highlighting match on via the `*.Dockerfile` glob.\n\n## Changes\n\n### 1. Move the two build files\n\n    git mv Dockerfile.api       docker/api.Dockerfile\n    git mv Dockerfile.ingester  docker/ingester.Dockerfile\n\nUse `git mv` so history follows the files. Their **contents are unchanged** \u2014\nevery path inside them (`COPY pyproject.toml uv.lock ./`, `COPY app ./app`,\n`COPY corpus/docs-initial/bulk/release-notes-4-1-de.pdf /tmp/warmup.pdf`) is\nrelative to the build context, not to the Dockerfile's own location, and the\ncontext stays `.`.\n\n### 2. Point compose at the new paths\n\n`docker-compose.yml` \u2014 two one-line edits, contexts untouched:\n\n```yaml\n  api:\n    build:\n      context: .\n      dockerfile: docker/api.Dockerfile   # was Dockerfile.api\n\n  ingest:\n    build:\n      context: .\n      dockerfile: docker/ingester.Dockerfile   # was Dockerfile.ingester\n```\n\n### 3. Update the two doc references\n\nFilename mentions only \u2014 no prose or decisions change:\n\n- `docs/work.md:146` \u2014 `` (`Dockerfile.ingester`) `` \u2192 `` (`docker/ingester.Dockerfile`) ``\n- `docs/work.md:148` \u2014 `` (`Dockerfile.api`) `` \u2192 `` (`docker/api.Dockerfile`) ``\n\n`docs/considerations.md:766` says \"a separate test-only Dockerfile/service\" \u2014\ngeneric, no filename, leave it. `README.md` and `.dockerignore` reference no\nDockerfile paths and need no change.\n\n## Verification\n\nFrom the repo root, unchanged commands must keep working:\n\n    docker compose build api ingest     # both images build from docker/*.Dockerfile\n    docker compose up -d                # api healthy on :8000, qdrant on :6333\n    docker compose logs -f api\n\nThen confirm the stack actually serves:\n\n    curl -sS localhost:8000/ -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\\n'    # expect 200\n\nAnd that the one-shot job still resolves its image and corpus mount:\n\n    docker compose run --rm ingest --dry-run   # prints the plan, exits 0\n\nSanity check that `.dockerignore` is still in force \u2014 the build context sent for\nthe `api` image should be megabytes, not gigabytes; watch the\n`transferring context:` line in the build output for a jump, which would mean\n`.venv/` or `corpus/` is being shipped.\n\nFinally, `git status` should show two renames (`R`) rather than an add+delete\npair, and `rg -n 'Dockerfile\\.(api|ingester)' --glob '!chat-logs'` should return\nnothing.\n"
}
```

**Result**
```
[project]
name = "chat-bot"
version = "0.1.0"
description = "Add your description here"
requires-python = ">=3.14"
dependencies = [
    "fastapi[standard]>=0.141.1",
    "pydantic-settings>=2.15.0",
    "anthropic>=0.116.0",
    "pyjwt>=2.13.0",
    "qdrant-client>=1.19.0",
    # Not ingestion-only: the api embeds the query before searching, and it must
    # use the same model as the corpus was indexed with. Brings torch with it.
    "flagembedding>=1.4.0",
]

[dependency-groups]
dev = [
    "matplotlib>=3.11.1",
    "pytest>=9.1.1",
    "pytest-playwright>=0.8.0",
    "python-docx>=1.2.0",
    "reportlab>=5.0.0",
]
# Parsing only. Docling drags in the layout, table-structure and OCR models on
# top of torch, and no request path touches any of it — so it stays out of the
# api image, which is the larger half of the split.
ingest = [
    "docling>=2.119.0",
]

[tool.uv]
# Local development gets everything. The api image installs main dependencies
# only, via `uv sync --no-default-groups`.
default-groups = ["dev", "ingest"]

[tool.pytest.ini_options]
pythonpath = ["."]
addopts = "-m 'not e2e and not docling and not embedding'"
markers = [
    "e2e: browser-driven Playwright tests. Run separately (`uv run pytest -m e2e`) — mixing them with anyio-marked tests in the same process breaks anyio's asyncio runner.",
    "ingest: ingestion pipeline tests (`uv run pytest -m ingest`, or `pytest tests/ingest`). Note a command-line `-m` replaces the whole filter in addopts, so exclusions must be spelled out in full: `-m 'not e2e and not docling and not ingest'`.",
    "docling: parses real documents with real models. Minutes, so out of the default run — `uv run pytest -m docling`.",
    "embedding: loads BGE-M3 and embeds for real. Several GB of weights, so out of the default run — `uv run pytest -m embedding`.",
]
=== hooks ===
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Exports the Claude Code chat log since the previous commit into chat-logs/
# and stages it, so every commit ships with the transcript that produced it.
#
# This file is tracked for reference/setup; the hook that actually runs
# lives at .git/hooks/pre-commit (git hooks are never tracked by git). To
# (re)install it in a fresh clone:
#   cp .githooks/pre-commit .git/hooks/pre-commit
#   chmod +x .git/hooks/pre-commit
set -e
cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"
python3 scripts/export_chat_log.py

=== app/main.py ===
from collections.abc import AsyncIterator
from contextlib import asynccontextmanager
from pathlib import Path

import anyio
from fastapi import FastAPI
from fastapi.staticfiles import StaticFiles

from app import vector_store
from app.config import get_settings
from app.embedding import get_embedder
from app.logging_config import configure_logging
from app.routers import api, pages

STATIC_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "static"


@asynccontextmanager
async def lifespan(app: FastAPI) -> AsyncIterator[None]:
    get_settings()  # fail fast on missing or invalid configuration
    configure_logging("api")
    vector_store.check_connection()  # fail fast if the vector store is unreachable
    # Load BGE-M3 now rather than on the first search. The weights are baked into
    # the image, but loading them still takes seconds — paid once at startup here
    # instead of by whoever asks the first question. Off the event loop, since it
    # is CPU-bound.
    await anyio.to_thread.run_sync(get_embedder)
    yield


app = FastAPI(title="chat-bot", lifespan=lifespan)
app.mount("/static", StaticFiles(directory=STATIC_DIR), name="static")
app.include_router(pages.router)
app.include_router(api.router)
=== app/rag/ingest/__init__.py ===
"""Ingestion: discovery, parsing, image descriptions, chunking and indexing.

Runs in its own container. Nothing here is imported by the api — Docling and
everything it drags in are absent from that image by design.
"""
=== app/routers/pages.py ===
from pathlib import Path

from fastapi import APIRouter, Request
from fastapi.responses import HTMLResponse
from fastapi.templating import Jinja2Templates

from app.security import issue_token

TEMPLATES_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "templates"

router = APIRouter()
templates = Jinja2Templates(directory=TEMPLATES_DIR)


@router.get("/", response_class=HTMLResponse)
def index(request: Request) -> HTMLResponse:
    """A fresh token per page render — the page is the only way to get one."""
    return templates.TemplateResponse(
        request, "index.html", {"chat_token": issue_token()}
    )

.claude/skills/e2e-testing/SKILL.md:41:`app.main:app` via `uvicorn` in a background thread on a free local port and
docs/work.md:26:- ~~Record TTFT and total completion per request, plus per-stage timers~~ (`app/timings.py`) — retrieval and generation are timed; rewrite/embed/search/expansion get timed as they are built, no collector change needed
docs/work.md:27:- ~~Split logging: ordinary logs at INFO, timings at DEBUG, separate files~~ (`app/logging_config.py`)
docs/work.md:28:- ~~API key and secret management: `.env` → OS env, no credentials in source~~ (`app/config.py`)
docs/work.md:30:- ~~API endpoint security: JWT embedded in the chat page, sent with every request, signature + TTL checked~~ (`app/security.py`) — opt-in per endpoint via `dependencies=[Depends(verify_token)]`; documented as a demonstration, not auth
docs/work.md:31:- ~~Validate and guard every turn, not just the latest~~ — validation in `app/routers/api.py` (shape, bounds, caps, leading-user rule), guarding in `app/guardrails.py` (question and every history turn, assistant turns included)
docs/work.md:32:- ~~Query rewrite step: structured output returning a query string, run before retrieval~~ (`app/rag/query_analysis.py`) — merged with an LLM safety verdict into one call; fails closed
docs/work.md:43:- ~~Turn `app/templates/index.html` into an actual chat interface~~
docs/work.md:72:- ~~Recursive walk for `.pdf`/`.docx`/`.html` at any depth, stable ordering~~ (`app/rag/ingest/discovery.py`) — skips dotfiles and the golden answer key
docs/work.md:74:- ~~Work out what a run has to do~~ (`app/rag/ingest/state.py`) — read from the collection rather than a side file, so the record cannot disagree with the index; new/changed/unchanged/deleted, with a multi-hash document treated as a partial write and re-ingested
docs/work.md:77:- ~~One Docling `DocumentConverter`, OCR on, table structure on, per-format options~~ (`app/rag/ingest/parse.py`) — built once per process; `generate_picture_images` on, or figure images are discarded and the caption stage has nothing to work with
docs/work.md:85:- ~~`run()` ties the pipeline together~~ (`app/rag/ingest/runner.py`) — discover → plan → parse → chunk → embed → index, one document at a time, returning a `RunReport`
docs/work.md:86:- ~~`python -m app.rag.ingest` over the `CORPUS_DIR` setting~~ (`app/rag/ingest/__main__.py`) — `--force` re-processes everything, `--dry-run` prints the plan and stops. No directory argument: the root is a setting, so a run cannot be aimed at arbitrary host files
docs/work.md:98:- ~~Chunk with `HybridChunker`~~ (`app/rag/ingest/chunk.py`) — BGE-M3's own tokenizer at `max_tokens=512`, table header repeated on overflow, peers merged. The tokenizer must be the embedder's: a chunk sized in someone else's tokens overflows silently at embed time
docs/work.md:102:- ~~Embed with BGE-M3, dense + sparse~~ (`app/embedding.py`) — both from one forward pass, so hybrid costs one model. Sparse is BGE-M3's own learned lexical weights, not BM25. Shared by the job and the api: a query embedded differently from its chunks silently fails to retrieve them
docs/work.md:106:- ~~Qdrant collection with named dense + sparse vectors~~ (`app/vector_store.py`) — both on one point, so a hybrid query prefetches each branch and fuses server-side; `doc_id` payload-indexed since every state read and delete filters on it. Verified against the real server, not just in-memory
docs/work.md:108:- ~~Dense distance is **dot**, not cosine~~ — identical while embeddings are unit-norm, and skips Qdrant's normalisation pass. It goes silently wrong if normalisation is ever turned off, so the embedding tests assert unit norm and `app/vector_store.py` carries the warning
docs/work.md:110:- ~~Index chunks as points~~ (`app/rag/ingest/index.py`) — `uuid5(doc_id, chunk_index)` ids so re-ingesting overwrites in place; payload carries the chunk text, so retrieval answers in one round trip and the api never needs the corpus mounted. `embed_text` is not stored — it exists only to be vectorised
docs/work.md:114:- ~~Hybrid retrieval via the Query API~~ (`app/rag/retriever.py`) — every populated branch searched on **both** vector kinds, fused server-side with RRF in one request; `retrieve()` returns `RetrievedChunk` with provenance, not strings. Prefetch (20) is wider than top_k (5): RRF can only rank what the branches surfaced
docs/work.md:123:- Enable citations on the generation call; fall back to a canned reply when a response carries none (TODO in `app/rag/llm.py`)
docs/work.md:130:- ~~Implement prompt guardrails on user input~~ (`app/guardrails.py`):
docs/work.md:135:- ~~Integrate the LLM via API, replacing the mocked responses~~ (`app/rag/llm.py`)
docs/work.md:136:- ~~Keep model configuration in one place~~ (`app/config.py`)
docs/work.md:145:- ~~Connect to Qdrant at api startup and fail fast if unreachable~~ (`app/vector_store.py`) — verified in the container: `qdrant connected url=http://qdrant:6333`
docs/work.md:166:- **Neighbour expansion** — retrieve the chunk, send it plus adjacent siblings. Deferred until the eval harness produces answers that are wrong *because a chunk was a fragment*: retrieval already finds the right document 18/18, so expansion currently has no failure to fix, and it would triple the context tokens to buy that. Trigger is measured failure, not preference. When it lands: neighbour ids are computable from `uuid5(doc_id, chunk_index)`, so it is a keyed `retrieve()` rather than a filtered scan; merge overlapping windows and clamp to `parent_id`. `point_id()` has to move out of `app/rag/ingest/index.py` first — that module imports docling, which must not reach the api
docs/considerations.md:474:Two layers, deliberately different in kind (`app/guardrails.py`):
docs/considerations.md:584:The rewrite call also returns a safety verdict (`app/rag/query_analysis.py`):
docs/considerations.md:592:**Why an LLM check on top of `app/guardrails.py`:** the regex heuristics there
docs/considerations.md:617:sanitised through `app/guardrails.py` before reaching it, and its system prompt
docs/considerations.md:661:`log_level` is a setting (`app/config.py`) and defaults to **DEBUG**, because
docs/requirements.md:143:- **Current state:** minimal FastAPI app (`app/main.py`, `app/routers/`) serving
docs/requirements.md:144:  the chat UI, with mocked retrieval and completion (`app/rag/`). Real RAG not
docs/requirements.md:172:- **Where it runs:** `app/rag/ingest/`, as a batch job — `python -m
docs/requirements.md:173:  app.rag.ingest`, or `docker compose run --rm ingest`. It takes **no** directory
docs/requirements.md:247:  (`app/rag/query_analysis.py`), so both jobs cost one round trip rather than
docs/requirements.md:256:- **Implemented:** plain HTML + vanilla JS (`app/templates/index.html`,
docs/requirements.md:257:  `app/static/`), no build step, served by the backend. Elements use accessible
docs/requirements.md:291:  as `Authorization: Bearer …` and `app/security.py:verify_token` checks

Import graph mapped. The natural split by actual usage:

- **common/** — `config`, `logging_config`, `request_context`, `embedding`, `vector_store` (the only five imported by *both* sides)
- **ingestion/** — all of `app/rag/ingest/*`, entrypoint becomes `python -m ingestion`
- **api/** — `main`, `routers/`, `static/`, `templates/`, plus `security`, `guardrails`, `timings`, `anthropic_client`, `rag/{retriever,llm,query_analysis}` (all api-only today)

Three decisions before I write the plan:

<details><summary>tool_use: AskUserQuestion</summary>

{
  "file_path": "/home/sebi/.claude/plans/hi-i-d-like-to-majestic-swan.md",
  "content": "# Split `app/` into `api/`, `ingestion/` and `common/`\n\n## Context\n\nEverything lives under a single `app/` package \u2014 the FastAPI request path, the\ningestion job, and the modules both of them share. The name says nothing (both\ncontainers are \"the app\"), and the boundary that actually matters is invisible:\n`app/rag/` mixes the query-time RAG path with `app/rag/ingest/`, a subpackage\nthat pulls in Docling and torch and must never be imported by the api.\n\nSplitting at the deployment boundary makes that structural. Three top-level\npackages, one per concern:\n\n| package | who runs it | contents |\n|---|---|---|\n| `common/` | both images | config, logging, embedding, vector store |\n| `api/` | api container | FastAPI app, routers, RAG query path, guardrails, auth |\n| `ingestion/` | ingest job | discovery, parse, chunk, index, runner, CLI |\n\nA side benefit worth having: each image copies only its own package plus\n`common/`, so api code stops shipping inside the ingester image and vice versa.\n\n**Scope rule** \u2014 `common/` holds only what *both* sides import today. `security`,\n`guardrails`, `timings` and `anthropic_client` are api-only right now and stay in\n`api/`; they move to `common/` if and when ingestion needs them.\n\n## Target layout\n\n```\ncommon/                  api/                     ingestion/\n  __init__.py              __init__.py              __init__.py\n  config.py                main.py                  __main__.py\n  logging_config.py        anthropic_client.py      discovery.py\n  request_context.py       guardrails.py            parse.py\n  embedding.py             security.py              chunk.py\n  vector_store.py          timings.py               index.py\n                           rag/                     state.py\n                             retriever.py           runner.py\n                             llm.py\n                             query_analysis.py\n                           routers/\n                             api.py\n                             pages.py\n                           static/\n                           templates/\n```\n\n`app/` disappears entirely.\n\n## 1. Move the files\n\n`git mv` throughout, so history follows. Move whole directories where possible\n(`app/rag/ingest` \u2192 `ingestion`, `app/routers`, `app/static`, `app/templates`,\n`app/rag/{retriever,llm,query_analysis}.py` \u2192 `api/rag/`).\n\nPackage markers: `app/__init__.py`, `app/rag/__init__.py` and\n`app/routers/__init__.py` are empty \u2014 reuse them for `api/__init__.py`,\n`api/rag/__init__.py`, `api/routers/__init__.py`, and add an empty\n`common/__init__.py`. `app/rag/ingest/__init__.py` carries a docstring and moves\nto `ingestion/__init__.py`; its wording (\"Runs in its own container. Nothing here\nis imported by the api\") is now enforced by the layout, so it stays as-is.\n\nNo code inside the moved files needs path fixes: `STATIC_DIR` in `main.py` and\n`TEMPLATES_DIR` in `routers/pages.py` are both built from `Path(__file__)`, and\nthe assets move with them.\n\n## 2. Rewrite imports\n\nEvery `from app.\u2026` / `from app import \u2026` line. Apply these with the **Edit tool,\nfile by file** \u2014 not `sed`, so the diff stays reviewable:\n\n| old | new |\n|---|---|\n| `app.config`, `app.logging_config`, `app.request_context` | `common.\u2026` |\n| `app.embedding`, `app.vector_store`, `from app import vector_store` | `common.\u2026` |\n| `app.guardrails`, `app.security`, `app.timings`, `from app import anthropic_client` | `api.\u2026` |\n| `app.main`, `app.routers.\u2026`, `app.rag.{retriever,llm,query_analysis}` | `api.\u2026` |\n| `app.rag.ingest.X` | `ingestion.X` |\n\nRoughly 40 import lines across ~20 source files and ~20 test files. Note the\nodd ones out: `tests/ingest/test_runner.py:13` (`from app.rag.ingest import\n__main__ as cli` \u2192 `from ingestion import __main__ as cli`),\n`tests/api/test_image_boundary.py:19` (`import app.main`), and the several\nfunction-local imports inside tests (e.g. `tests/test_embedding.py:74,103`,\n`tests/ingest/test_chunk.py:173`, `tests/conftest.py:28`).\n\nThe `# noqa: E402` comments on imports in `parse.py`, `chunk.py`, `runner.py`\nand `embedding.py` must survive the rewrite \u2014 they sit below deliberate\npre-import setup.\n\n**Tests are not reorganised.** `tests/` keeps its current shape (`unit/`, `api/`,\n`ingest/`, `e2e/`); only import lines change. Mirroring the new package layout\nis a separate call to make later.\n\n## 3. Entrypoints and Docker\n\n- `run.sh:11` \u2014 `uvicorn app.main:app` \u2192 `uvicorn api.main:app`\n- `Dockerfile.api`\n  - `COPY app ./app` \u2192 `COPY api ./api` and `COPY common ./common`\n  - warmup `RUN`: `from app.embedding import \u2026` \u2192 `from common.embedding import \u2026`\n  - `CMD` \u2192 `uvicorn api.main:app`\n- `Dockerfile.ingester`\n  - `COPY app ./app` \u2192 `COPY ingestion ./ingestion` and `COPY common ./common`\n  - warmup `RUN`: `ingestion.parse`, `ingestion.chunk`, `common.embedding`\n  - `ENTRYPOINT` \u2192 `[\"python\", \"-m\", \"ingestion\"]`\n- `docker-compose.yml`\n  - api: `command` uses `api.main:app`; bind mount `./app:/app/app` becomes\n    `./api:/app/api` + `./common:/app/common`\n  - ingest: bind mount becomes `./ingestion:/app/ingestion` + `./common:/app/common`\n\n`/app` stays as the in-container WORKDIR and `LOG_DIR=/app/logs` is unchanged \u2014\nit is a filesystem path, unrelated to the Python package name. `.dockerignore`\nneeds no edit (it never named `app/`, and the new directories are included by\ndefault). `pyproject.toml` needs no edit either: `pythonpath = [\".\"]` already\nputs the repo root on the path, and `--no-install-project` means the images\nresolve the packages from the WORKDIR.\n\n## 4. Docs\n\nMechanical path updates only \u2014 no prose, no decisions, no history rewritten:\n\n- `docs/requirements.md` \u2014 lines 143\u2013144, 172\u2013173, 247, 256\u2013257, 291\n- `docs/considerations.md` \u2014 lines 474, 584, 592, 617, 661\n- `docs/work.md` \u2014 ~20 parenthesised path annotations\n- `.claude/skills/e2e-testing/SKILL.md:41` \u2014 `app.main:app` \u2192 `api.main:app`\n\nTwo spots need more than a prefix swap because the module *name* changes:\n`docs/work.md:86` and `docs/requirements.md:172-173` both say `python -m\napp.rag.ingest` \u2192 `python -m ingestion`. And `docs/work.md:166` refers to\n`point_id()` having to move out of `app/rag/ingest/index.py` \"first, because that\nmodule imports docling, which must not reach the api\" \u2014 update the path to\n`ingestion/index.py`; the constraint it describes is unchanged.\n\n## Verification\n\nImport graph first \u2014 the failure mode here is a missed import, and it is cheap\nto catch:\n\n    uv run python -c \"import api.main, ingestion.runner, ingestion.__main__\"\n    rg -n '\\bapp\\.(config|embedding|vector_store|logging_config|rag|routers|main|security|guardrails|timings|request_context|anthropic_client)\\b|from app import' --glob '!chat-logs' --glob '!docs'\n\nThe second command must return nothing. Confirm no stray `app/` directory or\n`.pyc` remains: `git status` should show renames, and `find . -name __pycache__\n-path '*/app/*'` should be cleaned up (stale `app/__pycache__` can mask a broken\nimport by satisfying it from bytecode).\n\nThen the suites, fast to slow:\n\n    uv run pytest                      # default set (unit + api)\n    uv run pytest -m ingest            # ingestion pipeline\n    uv run pytest -m e2e               # browser-driven, boots api.main:app\n\nLocal run, then the containers:\n\n    ./run.sh                                    # then curl localhost:8000 -> 200\n\n    docker compose build api ingest\n    docker compose up -d && docker compose logs -f api   # healthy on :8000\n    docker compose run --rm ingest --dry-run            # prints the plan, exits 0\n\nTwo checks specific to this refactor:\n\n1. **The api image must not carry ingestion code or Docling** \u2014 that separation\n   is the reason for the split:\n\n       docker compose run --rm --entrypoint python api -c \"import ingestion\" # must fail\n       docker compose run --rm --entrypoint python api -c \"import docling\"   # must fail\n\n2. **No top-level name collision** \u2014 `api` and `common` are generic names, so\n   confirm neither shadows an installed distribution:\n\n       uv run python -c \"import api, common; print(api.__file__, common.__file__)\"\n\n   Both must resolve inside the repo, not into `.venv/lib/`.\n"
}

## Approved Plan:
# Split `app/` into `api/`, `ingestion/` and `common/`

## Context

Everything lives under a single `app/` package — the FastAPI request path, the
ingestion job, and the modules both of them share. The name says nothing (both
containers are "the app"), and the boundary that actually matters is invisible:
`app/rag/` mixes the query-time RAG path with `app/rag/ingest/`, a subpackage
that pulls in Docling and torch and must never be imported by the api.

Splitting at the deployment boundary makes that structural. Three top-level
packages, one per concern:

| package | who runs it | contents |
|---|---|---|
| `common/` | both images | config, logging, embedding, vector store |
| `api/` | api container | FastAPI app, routers, RAG query path, guardrails, auth |
| `ingestion/` | ingest job | discovery, parse, chunk, index, runner, CLI |

A side benefit worth having: each image copies only its own package plus
`common/`, so api code stops shipping inside the ingester image and vice versa.

**Scope rule** — `common/` holds only what *both* sides import today. `security`,
`guardrails`, `timings` and `anthropic_client` are api-only right now and stay in
`api/`; they move to `common/` if and when ingestion needs them.

## Target layout

```
common/                  api/                     ingestion/
  __init__.py              __init__.py              __init__.py
  config.py                main.py                  __main__.py
  logging_config.py        anthropic_client.py      discovery.py
  request_context.py       guardrails.py            parse.py
  embedding.py             security.py              chunk.py
  vector_store.py          timings.py               index.py
                           rag/                     state.py
                             retriever.py           runner.py
                             llm.py
                             query_analysis.py
                           routers/
                             api.py
                             pages.py
                           static/
                           templates/
```

`app/` disappears entirely.

## 1. Move the files

`git mv` throughout, so history follows. Move whole directories where possible
(`app/rag/ingest` → `ingestion`, `app/routers`, `app/static`, `app/templates`,
`app/rag/{retriever,llm,query_analysis}.py` → `api/rag/`).

Package markers: `app/__init__.py`, `app/rag/__init__.py` and
`app/routers/__init__.py` are empty — reuse them for `api/__init__.py`,
`api/rag/__init__.py`, `api/routers/__init__.py`, and add an empty
`common/__init__.py`. `app/rag/ingest/__init__.py` carries a docstring and moves
to `ingestion/__init__.py`; its wording ("Runs in its own container. Nothing here
is imported by the api") is now enforced by the layout, so it stays as-is.

No code inside the moved files needs path fixes: `STATIC_DIR` in `main.py` and
`TEMPLATES_DIR` in `routers/pages.py` are both built from `Path(__file__)`, and
the assets move with them.

## 2. Rewrite imports

Every `from app.…` / `from app import …` line, rewritten in bulk with `sed` over
the tracked Python files:

| old | new |
|---|---|
| `app.config`, `app.logging_config`, `app.request_context` | `common.…` |
| `app.embedding`, `app.vector_store`, `from app import vector_store` | `common.…` |
| `app.guardrails`, `app.security`, `app.timings`, `from app import anthropic_client` | `api.…` |
| `app.main`, `app.routers.…`, `app.rag.{retriever,llm,query_analysis}` | `api.…` |
| `app.rag.ingest.X` | `ingestion.X` |

Roughly 40 import lines across ~20 source files and ~20 test files.

Order matters — **`app.rag.ingest` must be rewritten before `app.rag`**, or the
ingestion paths get eaten by the broader RAG rule. Run over `api common
ingestion tests scripts`:

```sh
files=$(git ls-files '*.py' | grep -vE '^(app|docs)/')
sed -i -E \
  -e 's/\bapp\.rag\.ingest\b/ingestion/g' \
  -e 's/^from app\.rag import ingest\b/import ingestion/' \
  -e 's/\bapp\.(config|logging_config|request_context|embedding|vector_store)\b/common.\1/g' \
  -e 's/^from app import (vector_store)$/from common import \1/' \
  -e 's/^from app import (anthropic_client)$/from api import \1/' \
  -e 's/\bapp\.(guardrails|security|timings|main|routers|rag)\b/api.\1/g' \
  $files
```

Then review the full diff before running anything — `sed` will happily rewrite a
match inside a string literal, a comment, or a docstring, and there are `app`
mentions in prose comments throughout. Check by hand:

- `tests/ingest/test_runner.py:13` — `from app.rag.ingest import __main__ as cli`
  becomes `from ingestion import __main__ as cli`; the first rule produces
  `from ingestion import __main__ as cli` only if the module path collapses
  cleanly, so verify this one line.
- `tests/api/test_image_boundary.py:19` — `import app.main  # noqa: F401`
- function-local imports: `tests/test_embedding.py:74,103`,
  `tests/ingest/test_chunk.py:173`, `tests/conftest.py:28`,
  `tests/ingest/test_runner.py:72`
- FastAPI's `app` *variable* (`app = FastAPI(...)`, `uvicorn app.main:app`,
  `lifespan(app: FastAPI)`) must not be touched — the `\bapp\.` anchor should
  spare it, but confirm in `api/main.py` and `tests/conftest.py`.

The `# noqa: E402` comments on imports in `parse.py`, `chunk.py`, `runner.py`
and `embedding.py` must survive the rewrite — they sit below deliberate
pre-import setup.

**Tests are not reorganised.** `tests/` keeps its current shape (`unit/`, `api/`,
`ingest/`, `e2e/`); only import lines change. Mirroring the new package layout
is a separate call to make later.

## 3. Entrypoints and Docker

- `run.sh:11` — `uvicorn app.main:app` → `uvicorn api.main:app`
- `Dockerfile.api`
  - `COPY app ./app` → `COPY api ./api` and `COPY common ./common`
  - warmup `RUN`: `from app.embedding import …` → `from common.embedding import …`
  - `CMD` → `uvicorn api.main:app`
- `Dockerfile.ingester`
  - `COPY app ./app` → `COPY ingestion ./ingestion` and `COPY common ./common`
  - warmup `RUN`: `ingestion.parse`, `ingestion.chunk`, `common.embedding`
  - `ENTRYPOINT` → `["python", "-m", "ingestion"]`
- `docker-compose.yml`
  - api: `command` uses `api.main:app`; bind mount `./app:/app/app` becomes
    `./api:/app/api` + `./common:/app/common`
  - ingest: bind mount becomes `./ingestion:/app/ingestion` + `./common:/app/common`

`/app` stays as the in-container WORKDIR and `LOG_DIR=/app/logs` is unchanged —
it is a filesystem path, unrelated to the Python package name. `.dockerignore`
needs no edit (it never named `app/`, and the new directories are included by
default). `pyproject.toml` needs no edit either: `pythonpath = ["."]` already
puts the repo root on the path, and `--no-install-project` means the images
resolve the packages from the WORKDIR.

## 4. Docs

Mechanical path updates only — no prose, no decisions, no history rewritten.
Same `sed` approach, restricted to `docs/*.md` and the skill file, followed by a
diff review (these files discuss the app in prose, so false positives are likelier
here than in the source):

- `docs/requirements.md` — lines 143–144, 172–173, 247, 256–257, 291
- `docs/considerations.md` — lines 474, 584, 592, 617, 661
- `docs/work.md` — ~20 parenthesised path annotations
- `.claude/skills/e2e-testing/SKILL.md:41` — `app.main:app` → `api.main:app`

Two spots need more than a prefix swap because the module *name* changes:
`docs/work.md:86` and `docs/requirements.md:172-173` both say `python -m
app.rag.ingest` → `python -m ingestion`. And `docs/work.md:166` refers to
`point_id()` having to move out of `app/rag/ingest/index.py` "first, because that
module imports docling, which must not reach the api" — update the path to
`ingestion/index.py`; the constraint it describes is unchanged.

## Verification

Import graph first — the failure mode here is a missed import, and it is cheap
to catch:

    uv run python -c "import api.main, ingestion.runner, ingestion.__main__"
    rg -n '\bapp\.(config|embedding|vector_store|logging_config|rag|routers|main|security|guardrails|timings|request_context|anthropic_client)\b|from app import' --glob '!chat-logs' --glob '!docs'

The second command must return nothing. Confirm no stray `app/` directory or
`.pyc` remains: `git status` should show renames, and `find . -name __pycache__
-path '*/app/*'` should be cleaned up (stale `app/__pycache__` can mask a broken
import by satisfying it from bytecode).

Then the suites, fast to slow:

    uv run pytest                      # default set (unit + api)
    uv run pytest -m ingest            # ingestion pipeline
    uv run pytest -m e2e               # browser-driven, boots api.main:app

Local run, then the containers:

    ./run.sh                                    # then curl localhost:8000 -> 200

    docker compose build api ingest
    docker compose up -d && docker compose logs -f api   # healthy on :8000
    docker compose run --rm ingest --dry-run            # prints the plan, exits 0

Two checks specific to this refactor:

1. **The api image must not carry ingestion code or Docling** — that separation
   is the reason for the split:

       docker compose run --rm --entrypoint python api -c "import ingestion" # must fail
       docker compose run --rm --entrypoint python api -c "import docling"   # must fail

2. **No top-level name collision** — `api` and `common` are generic names, so
   confirm neither shadows an installed distribution:

       uv run python -c "import api, common; print(api.__file__, common.__file__)"

   Both must resolve inside the repo, not into `.venv/lib/`.

```
</details>

diff --git a/docs/considerations.md b/docs/considerations.md

--- a/docs/considerations.md

@@ -362,17 +362,26 @@ the model fragments; large chunks ground well but retrieve poorly.
 | Approach | Trade-off |
 |---|---|
 | Same unit for both | Simplest; fragments may lack context |
-| **Neighbour expansion — chosen** | Retrieve the chunk, send it plus adjacent siblings. No side-store, predictable token cost |
+| **Neighbour expansion — chosen, not built** | Retrieve the chunk, send it plus adjacent siblings. No side-store, predictable token cost |
 | Parent-section expansion | Best grounding, but needs parent text stored outside the vector payload, and one large section can blow the latency budget |
 
 Parent expansion was the initial choice and then dropped: it requires keeping
 section text in a side store (storing it per-chunk would duplicate a section
 once per child), plus a size cap and a fallback for oversized sections.
 Neighbour expansion gets most of the grounding benefit with none of that, since
-neighbours are just other chunks already in Qdrant, fetched by a payload filter
-on (`doc_id`, `chunk_index` range) — no extra embedding work at query time. It
-does need overlapping windows merged, and the window clamped to `parent_id` so a
-neighbour from a different section can't mislead grounding.
+neighbours are just other chunks already in Qdrant — and because point ids are
+`uuid5(doc_id, chunk_index)`, they are fetched by key rather than by a filtered
+scan, with no extra embedding work at query time. It does need overlapping
+windows merged, and the window clamped to `parent_id` so a neighbour from a
+different section can't mislead grounding.
+
+**It is designed and deliberately not built.** Retrieval finds the right
+document for 18 of 18 golden questions, so there is no observed failure that a
+wider window would fix, and expansion would roughly triple the context tokens to
+buy it. The trigger is an eval case that answers wrongly *because the chunk was
+a fragment* — the same rule applied to the captioner and to re-ranking: measured
+failure, not preference. `parent_id` is already in the payload, so the work is
+ready to start the moment such a case appears.
 
 This composes well with Claude's native citations: citations resolve to spans
 *within* whatever block is sent, so an expanded window costs no attribution
@@ -462,7 +471,7 @@ just the newest.

The refactor is complete and verified locally while the images build. Summary of what landed:

**Structure** — `app/` is gone, replaced by three top-level packages:

| package | contents |
|---|---|
| `common/` | `config`, `logging_config`, `request_context`, `embedding`, `vector_store` — the five modules both sides import |
| `api/` | `main`, `routers/`, `static/`, `templates/`, `rag/{retriever,llm,query_analysis}`, plus `guardrails`, `security`, `timings`, `anthropic_client` |
| `ingestion/` | `discovery`, `parse`, `chunk`, `index`, `state`, `runner`, `__main__` — entrypoint is now `python -m ingestion` |

All 24 moves recorded as git renames, so history follows.

**Beyond the import rewrite**, four things `sed` missed and I fixed by hand: `from app import vector_store` where a trailing `# noqa: E402` broke the anchor (`tests/conftest.py`, `ingestion/runner.py`), six `monkeypatch.setattr("app.anthropic_client...")` string literals, and two docstring path references. I also re-sorted 11 import blocks that the prefix change left out of alphabetical order — there's no ruff/isort configured, so that was manual.

**Left deliberately unchanged**: the `app` / `app.performance` / `app.ingest` logger names and `logs/app.log`, which are runtime identifiers rather than module paths; `/app` as the container WORKDIR and `LOG_DIR=/app/logs`; and `tests/`, which keeps its current shape (`unit/`, `api/`, `ingest/`, `e2e/`) — mirroring the new packages is a separate decision.

**Verified**: 229 default tests, 77 ingest, 11 e2e all pass. No residual `app.`/`app/` module references anywhere in source, docs, Docker or scripts. `api` and `common` both resolve inside the repo, not into `.venv/`.

Still outstanding: `docker compose build api ingest` is running in the background — I'll report when it finishes, then check that the api image genuinely refuses `import ingestion` and `import docling`, which is the separation this split was for. Note the two Dockerfiles remain at the repo root; we dropped that earlier move.

=== routers/api.py ===
import logging
from collections.abc import AsyncIterator
from typing import Literal

from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, Response
from fastapi.responses import PlainTextResponse, StreamingResponse
from pydantic import BaseModel, Field, field_validator

from api.rag.llm import stream_completion
from api.rag.query_analysis import AnalysisUnavailable, analyse_query
from api.rag.retriever import RetrievalQueries, retrieve
from api.security import verify_token
from api.timings import create_timings
from common.logging_config import APP_LOGGER, truncate
from common.request_context import new_request_id, set_request_id

router = APIRouter(prefix="/api")

logger = logging.getLogger(APP_LOGGER)

# Content decisions, not protocol errors, so both come back as a normal 200.
# Kept distinct: a user who asked something legitimate should not be told they
# did something wrong just because we broke.
REFUSAL_REPLY = "I can't help with that request."
UNAVAILABLE_REPLY = "Sorry — I couldn't process that just now. Please try again."

# The client cannot tell a refusal from an answer by the body alone, and must not
# record a refused exchange into history — otherwise the next request is judged
# against a conversation containing the attack, and one refusal poisons the
# session.
OUTCOME_HEADER = "X-Chat-Outcome"

# Conversation history is stateless: the client sends prior turns, so the whole
# history is caller-supplied and every turn is validated, not just the latest
# (requirements.md §6.4). Caps bound cost and context growth.
MAX_HISTORY_TURNS = 10
MAX_HISTORY_CHARS = 10_000
MAX_TURN_CHARS = 4_000


class Turn(BaseModel):
    role: Literal["user", "assistant"]
    content: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=MAX_TURN_CHARS)

    @field_validator("content")
    @classmethod
    def content_must_not_be_blank(cls, value: str) -> str:
        stripped = value.strip()
        if not stripped:
            raise ValueError("turn content must not be blank")
        return stripped


class ChatRequest(BaseModel):
    message: str = Field(min_length=5, max_length=300)
    history: list[Turn] = Field(default_factory=list, max_length=MAX_HISTORY_TURNS)

    @field_validator("message")
    @classmethod
    def message_must_not_be_blank(cls, value: str) -> str:
        stripped = value.strip()
        if not stripped:
            raise ValueError("message must not be blank")
        return stripped

    @field_validator("history")
    @classmethod
    def history_must_start_with_a_user_turn(cls, turns: list[Turn]) -> list[Turn]:
        """The Messages API requires the first message to be `user`. Rejecting
        here turns a forged history into our 422 rather than Anthropic's 400."""
        if turns and turns[0].role != "user":
            raise ValueError("history must start with a user turn")
        return turns

    @field_validator("history")
    @classmethod
    def history_must_be_within_total_length(cls, turns: list[Turn]) -> list[Turn]:
        total = sum(len(turn.content) for turn in turns)
        if total > MAX_HISTORY_CHARS:
            raise ValueError(f"history must not exceed {MAX_HISTORY_CHARS} characters")
        return turns


def _headers(request_id: str, outcome: str) -> dict[str, str]:
    # X-Request-ID ties a reply to its log lines; X-Chat-Outcome tells the client
    # whether this exchange belongs in the conversation history.
    return {"X-Request-ID": request_id, OUTCOME_HEADER: outcome}


def _log_completion(outcome: str, query: str, turns: int, timings, chunks: int = 0) -> None:
    logger.info(
        "chat request completed outcome=%s message_length=%d chunks=%d history_turns=%d",
        outcome,
        len(query),
        chunks,
        turns,
    )
    timings.log(message_length=len(query), history_turns=turns, outcome=outcome)


async def _stream_answer(
    query: str,
    turns: list[dict[str, str]],
    analysis,
    request_id: str,
    timings,
) -> AsyncIterator[str]:
    # Re-set inside the generator: it is iterated after the endpoint returns, so
    # this guarantees every downstream stage logs under the same id.
    set_request_id(request_id)

    queries = RetrievalQueries(
        original=query,
        rewritten=analysis.rewritten_query,
        keywords=tuple(analysis.keywords),
        sub_queries=tuple(analysis.sub_queries),
    )

    with timings.stage("retrieval"):
        chunks = await retrieve(queries)

    logger.debug(
        "stage=retrieval branches=%s chunks=%d sources=%s preview=%r",
        ",".join(queries.branches()),
        len(chunks),
        ", ".join(chunk.citation() for chunk in chunks) or "none",
        truncate(" | ".join(chunk.text for chunk in chunks)),
    )

    # TODO(citations): pass the chunks as document blocks instead of flattening
    # to text, so Claude's native citations resolve to a chunk rather than a
    # number the model wrote itself. See docs/work.md, RAG — Generate.
    context = [chunk.text for chunk in chunks]

    reply_chars = 0
    streamed = 0
    with timings.stage("generation"):
        async for chunk in stream_completion(
            query, context, history=turns, on_usage=timings.usage_recorder("generation")
        ):
            timings.mark_first_token()
            reply_chars += len(chunk)
            streamed += 1
            yield chunk

    logger.debug(
        "stage=generation reply_chars=%d chunks_streamed=%d", reply_chars, streamed
    )
    _log_completion("answered", query, len(turns), timings, chunks=len(context))


@router.post("/chat", dependencies=[Depends(verify_token)])
async def chat(request: ChatRequest) -> Response:
    """Analysis runs here rather than inside the generator: response headers are
    sent before the generator is iterated, so the outcome has to be known first.
    """
    request_id = new_request_id()
    set_request_id(request_id)
    logger.info(
        "chat request received message_length=%d history_turns=%d",
        len(request.message),
        len(request.history),
    )

    query = request.message
    turns = [turn.model_dump() for turn in request.history]
    timings = create_timings()
    logger.debug("stage=input message=%r history_turns=%d", truncate(query), len(turns))

    # Safety verdict and query rewrite share one call, before retrieval.
    analysis = None
    with timings.stage("analysis"):
        try:
            analysis = await analyse_query(
                query, turns, on_usage=timings.usage_recorder("analysis")
            )
        except AnalysisUnavailable:
            logger.exception("query analysis unavailable — refusing (fail closed)")

    # Fail closed: without a verdict we do not retrieve and do not generate.
    if analysis is None:
        timings.mark_first_token()
        _log_completion("unavailable", query, len(turns), timings)
        return PlainTextResponse(
            UNAVAILABLE_REPLY, headers=_headers(request_id, "unavailable")
        )

    if not analysis.safe:
        logger.warning("refused unsafe request category=%s", analysis.category)
        timings.mark_first_token()
        _log_completion("refused", query, len(turns), timings)
        return PlainTextResponse(
            REFUSAL_REPLY, headers=_headers(request_id, "refused")
        )

    return StreamingResponse(
        _stream_answer(query, turns, analysis, request_id, timings),
        media_type="text/plain",
        headers=_headers(request_id, "answered"),
    )
```
</details>

=== config.py ===
from functools import lru_cache
from pathlib import Path

from dotenv import load_dotenv
from pydantic import SecretStr
from pydantic_settings import BaseSettings, SettingsConfigDict

# `.env` is loaded into the OS environment; values already present there win,
# so real deployments can configure the app without shipping a file.
load_dotenv()


class Settings(BaseSettings):
    """Application configuration, read from the OS environment.

    The single place model and service configuration lives — secrets are never
    hardcoded in source (requirements.md §6.3, §7.3).
    """

    model_config = SettingsConfigDict(extra="ignore")

    anthropic_api_key: SecretStr
    anthropic_model: str = "claude-haiku-4-5"
    qdrant_url: str = "http://localhost:6333"
    qdrant_collection: str = "chunks"

    # One secret for the whole app, supplied via `.env`. Every process that
    # serves the app must share it, or tokens minted by one are rejected by
    # another.
    jwt_secret: SecretStr
    jwt_ttl_seconds: int = 1800

    # Extra retrieval branches from the rewrite. Both are unproven — switch them
    # off to A/B against the eval harness once a corpus exists.
    rewrite_keywords_enabled: bool = True
    rewrite_sub_queries_enabled: bool = True

    # Ingestion root, walked recursively. Deliberately the initial set rather
    # than all of `corpus/`: the later batch is copied in to demonstrate
    # incremental ingestion, and the golden answer key stays outside the tree.
    corpus_dir: Path = Path("corpus/docs-initial")

    # One model for both index-time and query-time embedding — they must never
    # drift, so this setting feeds the chunker's tokenizer and the embedder
    # alike. Chunks are sized in BGE-M3's own tokens; 512 rather than its 8192
    # ceiling, because a large chunk dilutes the embedding and costs precision.
    embedding_model: str = "BAAI/bge-m3"
    # BGE-M3's dense width. Lives here rather than in `common/embedding.py` because
    # the collection schema needs it and the api must be able to read it without
    # importing the embedding stack — that would drag torch into the api image.
    embedding_dimensions: int = 1024
    chunk_max_tokens: int = 512
    # Above `chunk_max_tokens`: the chunker sizes the raw chunk, then prepends
    # the heading path, so the embedded text runs slightly longer. Anything at
    # or below the chunk budget would truncate those chunks silently.
    embed_max_tokens: int = 1024
    embed_batch_size: int = 16

    # Per-branch candidates before fusion, and how many survive it. Prefetch is
    # deliberately wider than top_k: RRF can only rank what the branches
    # surfaced, so a chunk missing from every branch's shortlist is unreachable.
    retrieval_prefetch_limit: int = 20
    retrieval_top_k: int = 5

    # DEBUG by default: per-stage timings are only measured at this level, and
    # this is a PoC whose latency breakdown is a deliverable.
    log_level: str = "DEBUG"
    log_dir: Path = Path("logs")
    # Caps the message/prompt/chunk text in DEBUG trace lines. Prompts are tiny
    # today; with real retrieved chunks they would swamp the log.
    log_max_chars: int = 2000


@lru_cache
def get_settings() -> Settings:
    """Return the process-wide settings, built once on first use."""
    return Settings()
=== main.py ===
from collections.abc import AsyncIterator
from contextlib import asynccontextmanager
from pathlib import Path

import anyio
from fastapi import FastAPI
from fastapi.staticfiles import StaticFiles

from api.routers import api, pages
from common import vector_store
from common.config import get_settings
from common.embedding import get_embedder
from common.logging_config import configure_logging

STATIC_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "static"


@asynccontextmanager
async def lifespan(app: FastAPI) -> AsyncIterator[None]:
    get_settings()  # fail fast on missing or invalid configuration
    configure_logging("api")
    vector_store.check_connection()  # fail fast if the vector store is unreachable
    # Load BGE-M3 now rather than on the first search. The weights are baked into
    # the image, but loading them still takes seconds — paid once at startup here
    # instead of by whoever asks the first question. Off the event loop, since it
    # is CPU-bound.
    await anyio.to_thread.run_sync(get_embedder)
    yield


app = FastAPI(title="chat-bot", lifespan=lifespan)
app.mount("/static", StaticFiles(directory=STATIC_DIR), name="static")
app.include_router(pages.router)
app.include_router(api.router)
=== vector_store head ===
"""Qdrant client, the startup connectivity check, and the collection schema.

The check **fails the app's startup** if Qdrant cannot be reached. Docker
Compose is the supported way to run this project, and there the database is a
declared dependency — an api that boots without its vector store would only
fail later, per request, with a worse error. Running outside Compose means
providing Qdrant yourself.

The collection is created by the ingest job, never by the api: the api only
reads, and a reader that quietly creates an empty collection would turn "the
corpus was never ingested" into "every question is unanswerable" — much harder
to diagnose.

Note this module is imported by the api, so it must not import
`common.embedding` — that would pull FlagEmbedding and torch into the api image,
which deliberately carries neither. Hence the vector width comes from settings.
"""
import logging

from qdrant_client import QdrantClient, models

from common.config import get_settings
from common.logging_config import APP_LOGGER

logger = logging.getLogger(APP_LOGGER)

# Named vectors: one point carries both, so a hybrid query prefetches each
# branch and fuses server-side rather than hitting two collections.
DENSE_VECTOR = "dense"
SPARSE_VECTOR = "sparse"

# Dot product, NOT cosine — a deliberate choice that depends on an invariant.
#
# BGE-M3 is loaded with `normalize_embeddings=True`, and for unit-norm vectors
# dot and cosine are identical, so this costs nothing and skips Qdrant's
# normalisation pass at upsert.
#
# The catch: if embeddings ever stop being normalised — the setting flipped, a
# different model, a hand-inserted point — dot becomes silently wrong rather
# than erroring. Longer chunks carry larger norms and would rank higher
```
